G01 · Реализовано в макете
No pin/unpin controls, partition/order, per-project obligation counts or count-source inspector.

Что требовалось

Add sibling project/open and pin buttons; maintain pinned insertion order without duplicate cards; derive counts from the same unresolved Board fixture and link each count to exact scope.

Что сделано в целевом дизайне

  • Pin/unpin uses insertion order and a single card per project. Home counts and exact scope inspectors read the same live typed obligation union as Board and Inbox; read events do not resolve obligations.

G02 · Реализовано в макете
CEO floating launcher and pushed third column, shared project/estate manager conversation absent.

Что требовалось

Implement shell launcher/pane that narrows content; separate estate and project thread scopes, explicit no-model readiness and artifact-producing conversation fixture. Parent owns this fix.

Что сделано в целевом дизайне

  • Scoped floating CEO pane, draft/thread isolation, source-linked task artifact and explicit no-model/manual fallback. Agent-config artifact seeds an exact reusable configuration draft and navigates to its inspector.

G03 · Реализовано в макете
Summary lacks exact health dimensions, source age/coverage, register counts and source receipts.

Что требовалось

Render configured manager separately from observed detector/readiness; linked measured counts and per-source failures, including unavailable host.

Что сделано в целевом дизайне

  • Home independently reads scoped manager configuration and observed host/agent/session readiness, with exact source IDs, measured record counts, observation timestamp and independent unavailable states. Configuration and observation can fail separately.

G04 · Реализовано в макете
No starter choice/seed preview, portfolio observer target scope, staged configuration/activation or explicit PM gate.

Что требовалось

Add optional empty/software/observer branch with editable seeded objects, current/future-project scope confirmation, PM/resources/config review and blocked activation branch.

Что сделано в целевом дизайне

  • The optional creation disclosure exposes empty/software/repository/observer starter choice, editable initial stages, predefined role recommendations, addressed observer target and explicit future-project consent. The reviewed startupSpec is copied into the registered project, cycles remain paused, and PM activation retains its separate future-binding readiness gate. Stage values are entered in the disclosure; role recommendations are not editable role configuration.

G05 · Реализовано в макете
Repository radio does not control required selection; no primary repository, multiple drafts, create pending/unknown/reconciliation or stable draft identity.

Что требовалось

Use named fixture draft/project ids and draft tabs; repository choice updates readiness; show pending/refused/unknown/committed states using the same identity, plus cancel preserving or discarding only this draft.

Что сделано в целевом дизайне

  • Both creation URLs use one page-local Estate/draft authority. Named draft links retain exact draftId and inputs. Effective existing/no-repository choice is normalized before duplicate checks, review and commit. Frozen review/pending payloads, refusal, unknown-outcome reconciliation and committed identity preserve one request. Cancel/save preserves the addressed draft; discard affects only it and is held during pending outcome; replay does not create a second project and changed accepted payload is refused.

G06 · Реализовано в макете
After creation downstream setup routes are replaced with a summary and cannot finish first-run readiness.

Что требовалось

Carry the created project identity and inputs through its own providers/context/launch steps; allow completing the fictional readiness path without resetting to Atlas.

Что сделано в целевом дизайне

  • Creation registers an owned Project and selected repository, then continues to that exact Project guide and first Task; it borrows no Atlas task/run history. Deferred or absent provider creates no Builder, binding or session. An explicitly chosen provider creates an unverified candidate only; cycles remain paused and no TaskRun is created by creation or first Task recording. Existing context/runner/readiness/admission modules remain the subsequent path. Their older composed browser receipts are historical and do not establish acceptance of the new creation flow.

Routing alone does not compose custom notifier: routine+agent+delivery+template with schedule/event trigger; failure retries same frozen outbox body and honors retry_after.

Что требовалось

Create notifier and see it listed; simulate failure, preserve frozen message/window and retry eligibility; changing template cannot rewrite sent history.

Что сделано в целевом дизайне

  • Notifier composes routine+agent+channel+template and schedule/event trigger. notifier.created registers a concrete shared routine; frozen outbox/day/window survives template edit, quota admission and Retry-After guard same-message retry with explicitly advanced fixture time.

SRC-25 · Реализовано в макете
Project-settings revision saves need actual scoped data propagation, target-set validation and rollback-as-new-revision; proposal needs stale evidence refusal/refresh.

Что требовалось

Save Orbit name and see Orbit header update while Atlas unchanged; rollback creates a successor; stale proposal cannot resolve until refreshed.

Что сделано в целевом дизайне

  • Project settings initialize from the exact project, preserve drafts, preview field diff and expected revision, show the concurrent winner, require explicit rebase, and rollback as a successor revision. Saved name/purpose/repository/targets update canonical project and operational records. Reverse repo attach/primary/detach synchronizes ordered repositories and config_revision without stale form overwrite; live runs keep old pins. Stale Proposal evidence requires refresh before resolution.

Границы проверки документов

Этот реестр сохраняет исходный объём сверки макетов. Текущая достижимость R0 проверяется отдельно: аудит пользовательских путей и план снижения нагрузки. Число закрытых исходных строк не является приёмкой R0.

315 baseline paths имеют записанное назначение и SHA-256. Исторические и производные файлы отделены от действующих контрактов; это не заявление о повторном дословном прочтении каждого старого receipt. 15 сохранённых визуальных материалов просмотрены отдельно. Исходный план оператора сверен дополнительно.

docs/evidence/backlog.md:374

| M68 | **A declared bound on agent-to-agent loops** | the second example — developer finishes → validator runs → validator files fixes → developer — **is a cycle**. Two agents can hand each other work indefinitely, each step reasonable, the whole thing runaway. The bound belongs on the automation, declared at creation: after N rounds from one originating task the next result becomes a **proposal to the operator** instead of another task (ADR-0029's route). Discovered in production, this costs money for as long as nobody looks | **shipped** — the bound is on the CHAIN from one originating task, counted along `spawned` links only (`blocks` and `follows` order work rather than produce it). At the bound the next result becomes a proposal in the attention queue with two acts, and accepting creates the task WITHOUT reattaching it to the chain, because a person deciding is what makes it a new beginning rather than round five |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:389

| M73 | **Keep the computer awake** | three states: always / **while an agent is running** (the default) / never. It matters because a long session dies when the machine suspends, and the operator finds out hours later. **The distinction that decides whether this works: system sleep and display sleep are different.** `powerSaveBlocker.start('prevent-app-suspension')` keeps the machine awake and lets the screen go dark; `'prevent-display-sleep'` also burns the display for hours and is not what is wanted. macOS caveat to state in the UI rather than discover: with the lid closed on battery the machine sleeps regardless, so the setting must describe what it does instead of promising what the OS will not do | **shipped 2026-09-01** |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:401

| M74 | **One law for a citation** | plain click stays **inside**: a file opens in the editor, a URL opens in the embedded browser, an image or PDF opens in preview. **⌘-click goes outside**: a URL to the system browser, a file **revealed in Finder** (`shell.showItemInFolder`, not "open with default app"). One rule, everywhere, learned once | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:402

| M75 | **The embedded browser, and its isolation contract** | this is the **riskiest thing the product has done so far** — arbitrary remote content rendering inside an Electron app. Non-negotiable: `sandbox: true`, `contextIsolation: true`, `nodeIntegration: false`, **no preload at all**, its own `session.fromPartition()` so its cookies never touch the app's, `setWindowOpenHandler` denying popups, and a visible address bar so the operator always knows where they are. **Fabric never navigates it on its own initiative — only on the operator's click** | proposed |

docs/evidence/backlog.md:427

| M81 | **A diagnostic log across the whole product** | and the rule that keeps it honest: **the journal and the log are different planes and must never be confused.** The journal is what happened to the ESTATE — durable, replayable, gapless, and every row of it is evidence. The log is what the PROGRAM did — high volume, rotating, disposable, and losing it costs nothing. Structured JSONL in `userData/logs`, one file, renderer forwarded to main so there is a single timeline. **A correlation id on every IPC call and every MCP call**, so one click in a window can be followed through main to the database and back — that is the difference between a log you can search and a log you can only read. Redaction is part of the contract: never a bearer token, never a file's contents, never a memory claim in full. And a viewer in Settings → Diagnostics with a level filter, because "help us find problems" means the operator can look without opening a terminal | proposed |

| M104 | **A throw inside the MCP handler hangs the agent** | `createServer((req,res) => void this.handle(req,res))` — fire-and-forget, no `.catch`, and three reachable throws inside. | M110 | **No renderer tests, no CI, and six suites go green having run nothing** | `grep -riE 'jsdom\|vitest\|testing-library\|playwright'` across every `package.json` returns **nothing** — 2 800 lines of TSX with no seam. `index.ts` is unimportable (`app.whenReady()` at module scope), so `bootstrap`, `registerIpc`, all ~35 handlers, `reconcileOpenTasks` and `captureTranscript` are reachable only by launching Electron — `reconcile.test.mjs` says so in its own header and **re-implements the query instead of calling it**. The rest of `index.ts` is still reachable only by launching Electron, and "the file is unimportable" is recorded as a fact about the file rather than a reason |

docs/evidence/backlog.md:601

| M111 | **Cmd+W destroys every tab, and tabs are never persisted** | no `Menu` is installed, so Electron's default binds Cmd+W to Close Window, and `tabs` is plain component state. Five projects open, one reflex keystroke, the whole working set is gone with no undo and no restore on relaunch | **shipped** — Cmd+W closes the tab and Cmd+Shift+W the window, through a menu built from Electron’s ROLES so copy, paste and quit survive being replaced. The working set is written on every change and restored once the projects are known; a tab whose project is gone is dropped and said, and a draft is never restored because the tab without its content claims work that is not there |

An existing workspace repository may be named at initialisation and imported from instead of created. **Declining git is a supported answer, not a failure**: the workspace stays local, and the product says plainly that it then has no backup and no version history. Reconciles with ADR-0002 — declared data is mirrored to git, observed data is not — which decides WHAT may go in it | **shipped** — ADR-0002’s mirror, and now the question that offers it: choose a folder, adopt an existing workspace, or decline. Derived obligations enter the SAME scale (`refused` 40+access, `proposal` 35, `review` 25, `abandoned` 20, each plus project weight), so the Board is one list. `rankBoard` breaks ties deterministically — age then id — and the tiebreak test feeds the same pair in BOTH orders, because a `return 0` slipped through the one-directional version (V8’s sort is stable): the same class that bit M107. docs/evidence/backlog.md:693

| M155 | **The protocol is observed where it cannot be refused** | Three tiers, and naming them is the point: REFUSED (closing your own task, writing without a grant, handing off with no named thing, the loop bound), OBSERVED (rules never read, a review with no brief, a lease left held at session end), and ADVICE (the quality of a question or an insight, which nothing here can judge). Blocking on the unenforceable third is how a protocol becomes a workaround factory | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:694

| M156 | **A project's priority is declared, and pressure is measured beside it** | Tier — `critical` · `active` · `steady` · `paused` — is the operator's claim and is **never mutated by the system**; pressure (blocked tasks, oldest open question, a goal due within the week, recent activity) is measured and CAPPED, so it can lift a `steady` project above an idle `active` one and never above `critical`. Letting a busy project promote itself destroys the claim-versus-observation signal that makes the tier worth having (ADR-0002, ADR-0036). Plus `ceo_trust` on the estate with a per-project override that **may never exceed it** — ADR-0004's inheritance rule, not a second scale | **shipped** — `projectWeight` as a tested pure module: **tier + capped pressure**, where the cap is forced to (15,30) so a maxed `steady` project outranks an idle `active` one and never a bare `critical` one — a flat cap outside that window cannot satisfy both, which a test found before the code did. `ceoTrustFor` inherits the estate and clamps an override DOWN (ADR-0004 shape); an unknown level fails closed to `ask`. Storage: `priority_tier`/`priority_because`/`ceo_trust` on the project (tier NEVER mutated by the system — ADR-0002), one `estate_settings` row, `apply_priority` projector. P23 round-trips a declaration touching nothing else, a bad tier refused by the check, an override cleared, and the settings upsert. Four defects planted and caught |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:695

| M157 | **The CEO settles what it can CITE, and every settlement is visible and reversible** | A question carries `about` — a stable key naming its subject — and a decision fact with the same key answers it mechanically. That is the whole mechanism by which a CEO with no model answers anything. **A settlement with no basis is refused at every trust level**: an answer the CEO cannot cite is an answer it invented. Safety is citation, a standing “settled by the CEO” list, and one-press override — never a daily confirmation, which is a tax that trains the operator not to read | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:696

| M158 | **A decision that changes is scored, and the operator's own word is held** | `criticality = 40×(replaces a PERSON's decision) + 30×(needs a grant) + 20×(already released) + min(5×dependents,20) + project weight`. Above the threshold it becomes an `approval` question carrying the original, the replacement and the components. The first term puts any change to the operator's own decision beyond the CEO at any trust level — a decision the estate can overwrite is not a decision. A decision the CEO made carries no such term: the estate may revise its own reasoning freely | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:697

| M159 | **The Telegram connection: one token, one consumer, and every update claimed** | Token in the keychain with the service key's custody — main process only, never in a session bundle or the renderer. **Long polling, never a webhook**: we have no public endpoint, and `getUpdates` stops working the moment a webhook is set, which is a trap for whoever wires the other half later. `allowed_updates` names every type, because the default silently drops three. And the claim on `update_id` by INSERT — the only idempotency key Telegram gives, and **zero of eight live bots on this machine use it** (ADR-0037) | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:698

| M160 | **Authority requires a binding, and binding starts in the app** | Anyone in a group can message the bot, and answering a Board question settles estate state. A short-lived code issued in the app is redeemed in a **direct message** — starting from Telegram would let anyone claim to be the operator by asserting it, and redeeming in a group publishes the code to every member. Refusals leak nothing, not even that the estate exists | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:699

| M161 | **The outbox: a row before a send, `retry_after` honoured exactly** | `queued → sent → confirmed`, with Telegram's `message_id` kept because editing later needs both ids. Per-chat pacing against ~1/s and the 20-per-minute group ceiling. A failed message is retried FROM ITS ROW: regenerating a digest an hour later produces a different digest, and the operator receives two letters that disagree. Plus `route` and `mute` as two switches — where a kind goes, and whether a source may raise it — because one per-project toggle collapses two questions into one; and a mute may **never** cover `refused` | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:700

| M162 | **Reply-to-answer — the operator's day moves to Telegram** | The bot posts a question; the operator replies to that message; `reply_to_message.message_id` identifies it exactly. **No parsing, no model, no ambiguity** — this is the finding that decided the build order, because the loop the operator described works with no provider at all. Plus the structured commands, because a command is a contract and a sentence is an interpretation | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:701

| M163 | **The evening letter, labelled with the window it covers** | Per project then total: finished, started, sessions and outcomes, questions asked and answered, decisions, what is blocked and for how long. Composed by arithmetic. **We cannot promise a time** — nothing runs when the laptop is closed — so a missed letter is sent late and SAYS it is late, and two missed days are two letters. A day with nothing in it gets a letter saying so, or the operator learns to read absence as quiet rather than as broken | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:702

| M164 | **Notifiers are composed, not built** | `routine + agent + delivery + template`. Agents with their own instructions, schedules, triggers, agent-to-agent chains and gateway access ALL EXIST; the delivery step is the only new part, and composing means the notifier inherits the loop bound, the chain depth limit, the grant floor and the quota gate without one of them being rewritten. Created through a form today, a sentence when a provider lands — and the form is what the sentence would produce, not a placeholder for it | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:703

| M165 | **Capture, retention, and the consent that a work chat requires** | A bot cannot read history from before it joined, and reads a group at all only with privacy mode off — after which it receives EVERY message. So “analyse the thread” means analysing what we have been storing, which is a per-chat consent with a retention window, off by default. Built before the model tier because it is the expensive half and is useful alone as a record. A group is told less than a DM by default, and widening is an explicit journalled act | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:704

| M166 | **The CEO's core, and it is the whole CEO without a provider** | Hygiene, settlement by citation, criticality scoring and the digest arithmetic as pure modules in `shared/`, driven by the existing routine tick. **No loop, no framework, no key** — ADR-0038 decomposed the CEO and found four judgements among a dozen jobs, each of them a single typed transform rather than a goal pursued with tools. The product is complete at this step; everything after it is additive | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:705

| M167 | **A model port that may be ABSENT, and absence is a state rather than a crash** | A contract in `shared/` with a fake, so the core is tested against both a present and an absent provider. With none: the core runs unchanged, advisory calls are **skipped rather than stubbed** — a stub returns something, and something is what a person acts on — and the interface names which capabilities are unavailable instead of degrading quietly | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:706

| M168 | **The core is the checker, and a checker that never refuses is a finding** | Every advisory output is validated against facts before it reaches the Board: a proposed merge names two questions that exist and share a project, a drafted answer cites a fact that is current, a re-order moves within a stated band. **A refusal is journalled with its reason** — the stored verdict the orchestrator checklist asks for, and the evidence that lowers the CEO's trust level when a provider misbehaves | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:707

| M169 | **The provider router — and the three traps that are each a real bill** | Attempts capped **in total** rather than per provider, because three providers with three retries is nine calls for one prompt. An unhealthy provider probed **on a schedule**, because a check that only runs on failure never recovers and the chain runs one short with every request still succeeding. Failover **between turns only**, because reasoning carries a vendor credential and stripping it to move mid-turn is what produces the 400. Model, window and price at one boundary from configuration, never a table of vendor ids in source | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:708

| M170 | **The four typed calls, one at a time, each behind its validator** | `mergeSimilar`, `reorderWithReason`, `draftAnswer`, `readThread`. Each receives only what its transform needs and never the estate — a call handed everything has no window of its own. **None of the loop guards is implemented and that is a decision**: no tools, one turn, a typed value, so there is nothing to bound or trim. The day one of them gains a tool it becomes a loop and inherits every guard ADR-0038 declined | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:709

| M171 | **`model.called@1`, and the cost surface** | Provider, model, tokens and cost, journalled. The observable feed is then a view over a durable trace rather than the record itself — a stream nobody stored is a run that cannot be evaluated later. `quotaGate.ts` and `routine.ts` already prevent a CEO burning budget re-ranking a board nobody opened, so no new metering machinery is built | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:710

| M172 | `pi` as a third runner descriptor | Orthogonal to the CEO and cheap: `AGENTS` in `shared/agents.ts` is a descriptor list, and a third entry costs a program name, a surface adapter and its permission modes. **`pi` is a RUNNER, not a CEO host** — running the CEO on a coding-agent CLI hands a terminal, a working directory and file access to something whose whole job is to rank and cite. Not installed on this machine today | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:711

| M173 | **The decision graph shows movement, and who decided** | M144 shipped it as a list because there were no edges yet. The operator's ask — how the project reached here, human vs agent, and WHY an agent decided — needs no new storage: the decision fact carries `actor_kind` (person/agent/system), `superseded_by` carries the lineage, and the context lockfile (M49) carries `sha256` + `fact_ids` + `transcript_ids`, so “what did the agent know when it decided” is a JOIN, not a record. Human decisions are the spine, agent decisions branch off the work; clicking one opens the exact facts and transcript it was handed — evidence, because it could not have known more than the lockfile lists. Derived by query — a **script**, not an agent | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:712

| M174 | **The script-vs-agent line, drawn across the product** | The operator's rule: variable data wants an agent, a computable answer wants a script — it saves tokens and removes a class of error a script cannot make. The test: can the output be wrong in a way a type or a comparison catches? Then a script. Classified in `agent-system-map.md` §3: almost everything the CEO does is a script, and the reclassification of a script job to an agent is a decision with a token cost, made in the open rather than by adding one model call inside a pure function | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:713

| M175 | **The CEO is an agent — a small tool-calling loop, ours** | The operator chose flexibility and tools (ADR-0039, superseding ADR-0038 §1/§6). ~150 lines of `agent-orchestrator` §2 over the `ModelPort` router, with the four guards: in-loop trimming, wrap-up injection, a max-iteration guard, an iteration refund on recoverable errors. **Auditability moves from the shape of the run to the tools**: `settle_by_citation` refuses a basis-less settlement in the schema, and every tool call is journalled so the trajectory IS the audit trail. Dormant with no provider — the deterministic tools are the whole core | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review

| M177 | **Close the cheap harness gaps found by the map** | Four things the audit surfaced, none waiting on a provider: inject **today's date** into the context pack (a model with a cutoff answers staleness from memory), **enumerate the status vocabulary** in the protocol so the agent stops learning the allowed set by rejection, **use `actor_kind` in trust** so an agent-authored fact is not read as a person-authored one (the column exists, unused), and make the protocol **re-assertable after a CLI compaction** that drops it | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:716

| M178 | **A heartbeat, because silence is ambiguous** | `fabric_heartbeat({ phase, note?, waiting_on? })` — a positive signal, journalled as `agent.heartbeat@1`. Liveness today is `idle` after 60s of silence, and silence hides three cases with different answers: an agent THINKING, an agent WEDGED, and an agent WAITING on a Board question — all silent. `phase` is enumerated (an agent invents its own vocabulary otherwise) and `waiting_on` names what a blocked agent waits on, turning silence into a fact. Five liveness states replace three, and only `stalled` claims a fault (ADR-0040) | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:717

| M179 | **The agent is watched from outside, because the reporter is what breaks** | A heartbeat is the agent's own account; the thing most likely broken is the reporter. Fabric watches the process (`onExit`), the heartbeat gap (a subtraction against a threshold), and **the absence of `context.read`** — every session is told to call `fabric_whoami` first and that call is journalled, so a session producing output that never read its rules is running WITHOUT the harness: a `harness.break@1` finding with the reason it can infer. Nothing surfaces this today | proposed |

requires_full_semantic_review
docs/evidence/backlog.md:718

| M180 | **A failure is journalled with its KIND — “failed” is not one thing** | Runner-missing, skill-failed-to-load, harness-unreachable, agent-crashed, agent-wedged, task-failed-HONESTLY, advisory-model-failed, notifier-source-down — each a distinct detector and remedy, and an undeterminable kind is `unknown` and says so. M100's startup lesson generalised: name the precondition, or admit we do not know. A task that failed honestly is `review` with the answer “no”, NOT a crash — conflating them discards a real result | proposed |

The model absent: the CEO runs its deterministic tools and names the advisory capabilities as unavailable. A notifier that cannot gather: it sends what it has with a partial note, or nothing with a recorded reason. A degradation the operator cannot see is a lie by omission — every path here states it. A `stalled` or `harness-break` agent enters the same Board queue as everything else (ADR-0040) | proposed |

The three service categories are the raw material of Fabric's own product backlog, and today they drown in project memory where nobody sweeps across projects (ADR-0041) | proposed |

| M195 | **Redaction at the agent surface — S1, HIGH** | `redact()` is applied to transcripts and NOT to what agents write through tools: a secret written into `memory_facts` becomes current, enters EVERY next context pack, and multiplies through transcripts; a question with a secret reaches the Board and (R3) Telegram. Fix at the surface boundary for every agent-written text field (memory claim, note, brief, question text/options), plus a probe planting a token in each field and watching the scrub, plus the mint-entropy check (S5) | **shipped** — and step 0 corrected the brief FOURFOLD: it named three fields in three tools; measuring found **thirteen across eight**, the most dangerous being `task.handoff@1`’s 8 000-char `value` — precisely the channel one agent uses to pass the next what it obtained. So the fix is ONE DOOR, not thirteen call sites (M106’s lesson one layer along): `appendRedacted` wraps every agent-written event, and all 15 raw appends in the surface now route through it. `redactPayload` walks the whole payload recursively — **no skip-list**, measured safe: nine ordinary values (ids, enums, `about` keys, formatted strings) came back byte-identical while the secret was caught. The count rides on the event, the transcript idiom. Token entropy verified at 192 bits (S5). Three defects planted, three caught |

docs/evidence/backlog.md:732

| M196 | **Sandbox on the windows — S2** | All three BrowserWindows run `sandbox: false`; the preload uses only `contextBridge`, so sandbox compatibility is likely. Enable it, or record in the window comment exactly why it cannot be — an undocumented `sandbox: false` reads as an oversight forever | **shipped** — and the brief’s guess (“sandbox: true is likely compatible”) was wrong TWICE, both found by driving a real window. First: an ESM preload dies in a sandbox with `SyntaxError: Cannot use import statement outside a module` and `window.fabric` is **ABSENT** — the renderer loses all 27 namespaces silently, so the sandbox was off for a REASON nobody had recorded. Rebuilt as CJS, it failed a second way: in CJS format the resolver picks the **electron npm wrapper** — the one that `spawnSync`s the binary — dragging `child_process` into a context that has none. `external: ['electron']` is what makes the sandbox possible at all. Both halves are now held by a probe reading the SHIPPED bundle (CJS, no forbidden builtin, bridge still exposed, all 3 windows sandboxed); three regressions planted, three caught |

It arrived verbatim from migration 3 through every legacy copy, so it predates the refactor that found it, and the refactor journalled it rather than fixing it inside a diff that must contain no behaviour change. **P24 excludes the column and says why**, so the exclusion is a recorded finding rather than a convenience. docs/evidence/specs/2026-08-16-software-fabric-carryover.md:60

| CO-049 | post stage 1 | **The shared record plane is a Notion adapter that does not exist yet.** Two people on two machines need shared awareness — who holds what right now, the board, cross-repo signals — and `agent-sync` ships exactly two backends, `outline` and `fs` (`agent_sync.py:4470`). Operator chose 2026-08-25 to write a Notion backend rather than pay for Outline Cloud ($10/month for 1–10 members, getoutline.com/pricing) or share a corporate wiki. Notion can satisfy the contract honestly — `PATCH /v1/blocks/{id}/children` is a server-side append, so `atomicAppend` need not be a lie — under ~3 req/s per connection plus a per-workspace limit | the adapter is code in the `agent-sync` repository, with tests, not a setting in this one | before a second person works this repository, and before `reserve` is relied on | `agent-sync` repo | **resolved 2026-08-26** — built, measured and released as `agent-sync` 1.18.0. The flags are earned, not read off the docs: 200 appends from two processes left 200 lines on one page, two reads returned one order, and two freshly created shards were both enumerated — the Outline defect that cost eight processes eight winners. npm serves 1.18.0 and this machine's plugin is on it. Pointing **fabric's own** plane at `notion` is done too, 2026-08-26: `bootstrap` created the container, the baseline was re-stamped on the new plane (`ADR-0010` / `CO-050`), `check` reports `setup healthy (11 checks passed)`, and `reserve ADR` returned `ADR-0011` — the one thing `fs` could never do. Record plane `notion`, lease on git refs: exclusive across machines, which is what two people on two machines needed. The old `fs` plane's local logs do NOT migrate; they stay in `.agent-sync/`, gitignored, and the new plane starts from the re-stamped baseline |

| CO-050 | post stage 1 | **The guard hook does not cover Bash.** `PreToolUse` matches `Edit\\|Write\\|MultiEdit\\|NotebookEdit` and `Bash(git commit *)` (plugin `hooks/hooks.json`), so a guarded file written with `cat >` or `sed -i` is never checked. Every edit in the 2026-08-25 initialisation run went that way; the lease was held and `guard` run by hand, which is the self-check the skill prescribes outside Claude Code — not enforcement | the matcher belongs to `agent-sync`, and widening it to all Bash means parsing shell to find write targets | before this repository is described to anyone as protected | `agent-sync` repo | open — **filed upstream 2026-08-26 as AS-06** on `agent-sync`'s board, with the evidence: `GUARD_SHAPES` covers three `git commit` shapes and no direct Bash write. A second one was found beside it and filed as AS-07 — `hooks/_lib.sh:36` resolves the project as `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$PWD}`, so a lease in a SECOND repository is never renewed; this run's own lease expired 14 minutes before its work ended |

| CO-090 | memory-adr-sec-wave1 | **Same-uid isolation has a floor, and wave 1 raised it rather than removing it.** Every session runs as the operator, so an `mcp.json` at 0600 is readable by the next agent. | CO-093 | memory-adr-sec-wave1 | **The bundle path has never been exercised by a real Claude Code session, and this run could not exercise it either.** Three attempts inside the sandbox produced zero requests at the surface — the nested CLI never loaded `--mcp-config` at all, so nothing was learned about the handshake either way. | CO-096 | project-page | **Where does a token and cost number come from?** M57 wants tokens and spend on the strip. Claude Code does not report its own usage over the MCP surface, and reading it out of the transcript is parsing a UI. Candidates: the provider's usage API per key, a proxy in front of the session, or accepting that cost is per-estate and not per-project. Until one is chosen the strip must not show a number it cannot source | operator review 2026-09-01 | before M57 ships a cost figure | stage 2 | **RESOLVED 2026-09-01, and the answer is no.** Measured live against the endpoint `claude-swap` uses: `GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage`, bearer from the macOS Keychain (service `Claude Code-credentials` → `claudeAiOauth.accessToken`; on macOS it is NOT in `~/.claude/.credentials.json`), header `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20`. HTTP 200. It returns `five_hour.utilization: 46.0` with `resets_at`, `seven_day.utilization: 17.0`, per-model `seven_day_opus`/`seven_day_sonnet`, and `extra_usage {monthly_limit, used_credits, currency}`. **`limit_dollars`, `used_dollars` and `remaining_dollars` all came back `None`** on this plan, and nothing in the response is per-session or per-project. So: **the per-project cost cell has no source and is dropped** — not zero, not a dash, absent. What exists instead is an ESTATE-level quota reading, which is M83 and is arguably the more useful number. Three caveats recorded with it: the endpoint is undocumented and internal to the Claude Code client, so it must degrade by showing the last-known value WITH ITS AGE rather than a stale number as current; reading it means the app now holds an OAuth token that can act as the operator, which must never be logged (M81 redaction), must go nowhere but `api.anthropic.com`, and must be disclosed in the interface; and polling must back off and honour `Retry-After` |

| CO-100 | project-page | **What does "instance" count?** M69 shows how many instances of each agent a project has. | CO-102 | project-page | **Does the embedded browser share the operator's logged-in sessions?** It cannot: Electron cannot read Safari's or Chrome's cookies, and giving it the app's own session would mix a web page's storage with the product's. That makes ⌘-click to the real browser load-bearing rather than a nicety, and it must be obvious in the UI rather than discovered.
